  [G10.5] Vulnerabilities recorded in the file and in the report
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The suitability report asserts that the client has no vulnerability: /vulnerability/perClient[]/hasVulnerability = 'No'. The report does not extract vulnerability categories, characteristics, or assessment date. Within the narrative, the report records the client's Attitude to Risk (rating 5, balanced investor), Capacity for Loss (acknowledges investment values can rise or fall, not reliant on pension funds, underperformance would not impact day-to-day living, receives State Pension approximately £230/week, works as HGV driver earning £300/week net, additional £20/week from Sea Cadets role, spouse contributes £1,100/month), and risk profile agreement. The report states 'It is important to continually review your risk profile moving forward to ensure that this level of risk remains in line with your personal attitudes'.
    File:     The file contains evidence from multiple categories: (C) File notes record ATR questionnaire discussion and capacity for loss assessment; (B) Fact Find includes age 70, gender male, marital status married, income details (net monthly £1,430 from employment, but total monthly expenditure £1,718, yielding disposable income of minus £288), and marketing preferences with 'No Requirement' for accessible format; (D) Risk profile report states 'No additional notes were recorded' for investor experience and capacity for loss questionnaire discussions; (H) Terms and conditions and Key Features documents provide standard product and process information; (A) Client Agreement and Privacy Notice; (F) Planning scenario showing assets and timeline with spouse involvement. No vulnerability assessment, vulnerability categories, vulnerability characteristics, or any recorded indicators of vulnerability (such as health issues, life events, low capability, low resilience, dependency, communication barriers, or support needs) appear anywhere in the case file.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report asserts no vulnerability (hasVulnerability = 'No') and the file contains no records of any vulnerability assessment, vulnerability indicators, or vulnerability-related concerns. This is internally consistent between report and file, but raises the question of whether a vulnerability assessment was conducted at all.
      - The report does not extract or disclose /vulnerability/perClient[]/categories[], /vulnerability/perClient[]/characteristics[], or /vulnerability/perClient[]/assessedOn. The extraction metadata confirms these are absent from the report ('NotPresentInDocument'). The file likewise contains no such information.
      - The Fact Find document records 'Accessible format requirement: No Requirement' [P10], which indicates a question was asked but reveals no accessibility need; this is consistent with no recorded vulnerability.
      - The Fact Find notes 'No additional notes were recorded' for investor experience and capacity for loss questionnaire discussions [P13], meaning the discovery process ran but generated no special considerations requiring disclosure.
      - The client's current monthly cash flow shows disposable income of minus £288 (net income £1,430 vs expenditure £1,718) [P9], indicating a potential financial stress or capacity-related characteristic. This negative disposable income is not addressed in the report's capacity for loss narrative, which states the client is 'not reliant upon your pension funds' and underperformance 'would not have a detrimental impact on day-to-day living'. The file does not flag this mismatch as a vulnerability requiring support or adjustment.
    The requirement asks whether vulnerabilities recorded in the file are reflected in the report, and whether vulnerabilities asserted in the report are supported by evidence. The canonical model shows the report asserts hasVulnerability = 'No' and does not populate vulnerability categories, characteristics, or assessment date. The file contains no independently recorded vulnerability assessment, no vulnerability indicators flagged in case notes, and no client request for support or adjustment. The Fact Find and Risk Profile documents show questions were asked (ATR questionnaire, Capacity for Loss questionnaire, accessible format preference) but the outcome was no recorded concerns. This represents internal consistency: report and file align on no vulnerability. However, two issues arise under the requirement's logic: (1) A negative monthly cash flow of minus £288 exists in the Fact Find [P9] but is never addressed, contradicted, or explained in relation to capacity for loss. The report's assertion that underperformance would not impact day-to-day living sits uneasily against expenditure exceeding income by £288/month, yet the file notes do not explore or resolve this. The disposal of this inconsistency (if it exists) is absent. (2) The extraction notes (expectedButAbsent section) confirm no vulnerability path data was found in the report, and the guard conditions note that a vulnerability the client asked not to have written in may still have been handled correctly. The lack of any vulnerability disclosure in the report is consistent with no vulnerability in the file, but the requirement obliges the assessor to check whether assessment occurred. The file contains no dated vulnerability assessment, no documented discussion of vulnerability, and no evidence that FG21/1 drivers (health, life event, low resilience, low capability) were systematically considered. The Capacity for Loss section in the report acknowledges investment risk and confirms the client is not reliant on pension funds, but does not address the negative cash flow or any other potential vulnerability driver. Under the decision logic, this is not a Potential Concern if no vulnerability exists and the file confirms none was found; it becomes a Potential Concern only if evidence of a vulnerability exists in the file but is omitted from the report, or if the report asserts a vulnerability without file support. Here, neither is the case. The report's silence on vulnerability is matched by the file's silence. Nevertheless, the unresolved negative cash flow and the absence of any dated, documented vulnerability assessment process leave the assessment incomplete: the file does not record why no vulnerability assessment concerns were identified despite the cash flow deficit.
